## Explanatory notes

- [Jump up to:1](#cite_ref-Problems_of_AI_2-0)[2](#cite_ref-Problems_of_AI_2-1)This list of intelligent traits is based on the topics covered by the major AI textbooks, including:[Russell&Norvig (2021)](#CITEREFRussellNorvig2021),[Luger&Stubblefield (2004)](#CITEREFLugerStubblefield2004),[Poole, Mackworth&Goebel (1998)](#CITEREFPooleMackworthGoebel1998)and[Nilsson (1998)](#CITEREFNilsson1998).
- [Jump up to:1](#cite_ref-Tools_of_AI_3-0)[2](#cite_ref-Tools_of_AI_3-1)This list of tools is based on the topics covered by the major AI textbooks, including:[Russell&Norvig (2021)](#CITEREFRussellNorvig2021),[Luger&Stubblefield (2004)](#CITEREFLugerStubblefield2004),[Poole, Mackworth&Goebel (1998)](#CITEREFPooleMackworthGoebel1998)and[Nilsson (1998)](#CITEREFNilsson1998).
- [↑](#cite_ref-26)Formal knowledge representations are used in content-based indexing and retrieval,[[19]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmoliarZhang1994-21)scene interpretation,[[20]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeumannMöller2008-22)clinical decision support,[[21]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKupermanReichleyBailey2006-23)knowledge discovery (mining "interesting" and actionable inferences from large[databases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database)),[[22]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGarry2005-24)and other areas.[[23]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBertiniDel_BimboTorniai2006-25)
- [↑](#cite_ref-33)A complete formal knowledge base needs to represent things such as objects, properties, categories, and relations between objects;[[25]](#cite_note-28)situations, events, states, and time;[[26]](#cite_note-29)causes and effects;[[27]](#cite_note-30)knowledge about knowledge (what we know about what other people know);[[28]](#cite_note-31)[default reasoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Default_reasoning)(things humans assume are true until they are told differently and will remain true even when other facts are changing);[[29]](#cite_note-Default_reasoning-32)and many other aspects and domains of knowledge.
- [↑](#cite_ref-37)Knowledge acquistion is among the reasons that[expert systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expert_system)proved to be inefficient for capturing knowledge.[[31]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTENewquist1994296-35)[[32]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrevier1993204–208-36)
- [↑](#cite_ref-38)"Rational agent" is general term used in[economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economics),[philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophy)and theoretical artificial intelligence. It can refer to anything that directs its behaviour to accomplish goals, such as a person, an animal, a corporation, a nation, or in the case of AI, a computer program.
- [↑](#cite_ref-52)[Alan Turing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Turing)discussed the centrality of learning as early as 1950, in his classic paper "[Computing Machinery and Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computing_Machinery_and_Intelligence)".[[44]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTETuring1950-50)In 1956, at the original Dartmouth AI summer conference,[Ray Solomonoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Solomonoff)wrote a report on unsupervised probabilistic machine learning: "An Inductive Inference Machine".[[45]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESolomonoff1956-51)
- [↑](#cite_ref-61)See[AI winter §Machine translation and the ALPAC report of 1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI_winter#Machine_translation_and_the_ALPAC_report_of_1966).
- [↑](#cite_ref-106)Compared with symbolic logic, formal Bayesian inference is computationally expensive. For inference to be tractable, most observations must be[conditionally independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conditionally_independent)of one another.[AdSense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AdSense)uses a Bayesian network with over 300million edges to learn which ads to serve.[[97]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomingos2015chpt._6-105)
- [↑](#cite_ref-109)Expectation–maximisation, one of the most popular algorithms in machine learning, allows clustering in the presence of unknown[latent variables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latent_variables).[[99]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomingos2015210-108)
- [↑](#cite_ref-136)Some form of deep neural networks (without a specific learning algorithm) were described by:[Warren S. McCulloch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_S._McCulloch)and[Walter Pitts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Pitts)(1943);[[120]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig202117-130)[Alan Turing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Turing)(1948);[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)[Karl Steinbuch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Steinbuch)and[Roger David Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_David_Joseph?action=edit&redlink=1)(1961).[[122]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._5-132)Deep or recurrent networks that learned (or used gradient descent) were developed by:[Frank Rosenblatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Rosenblatt)(1957);[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)[Oliver Selfridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Selfridge)(1959);[[122]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._5-132)[Alexey Ivakhnenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexey_Ivakhnenko)and[Valentin Lapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentin_Lapa?action=edit&redlink=1)(1965);[[123]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._6-133)[Kaoru Nakano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaoru_Nakano?action=edit&redlink=1)(1971);[[124]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._7-134)[Shun-Ichi Amari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shun-Ichi_Amari)(1972);[[124]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._7-134)and[John Joseph Hopfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Joseph_Hopfield)(1982).[[124]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._7-134)Precursors to backpropagation were developed by:[Henry J. Kelley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_J._Kelley)(1960);[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)[Arthur E. Bryson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_E._Bryson)(1962);[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)[Stuart Dreyfus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_Dreyfus)(1962);[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)[Arthur E. Bryson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_E._Bryson)and[Yu-Chi Ho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yu-Chi_Ho)(1969).[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)Backpropagation was independently developed by:[Seppo Linnainmaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seppo_Linnainmaa)(1970);[[125]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidhuber2022sect._8-135)and[Paul Werbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Werbos)(1974).[[121]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussellNorvig2021785-131)
- [↑](#cite_ref-138)[Geoffrey Hinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Hinton)said, of his work on neural networks in the 1990s, "our labeled datasets were thousands of times too small. [And] our computers were millions of times too slow."[[126]](#cite_note-137)
- [↑](#cite_ref-264)In statistics, a[bias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bias_(statistics))is a systematic error or deviation from the correct value. But in the context of[fairness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairness_(machine_learning)), it refers to a tendency in favour or against a certain group or individual characteristic, usually in a way that is considered unfair or harmful. A statistically unbiased AI system that produces disparate outcomes for different demographic groups may thus be viewed as biased in the ethical sense.[[251]](#cite_note-Samuel-2022-263)
- [↑](#cite_ref-275)Including[Jon Kleinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Kleinberg)([Cornell University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_University)), Sendhil Mullainathan ([University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago)), Cynthia Chouldechova ([Carnegie Mellon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnegie_Mellon)) and Sam Corbett-Davis ([Stanford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford))[[261]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChristian202067–70-274)
